# Board spike

A throwaway spike. It answers one question with measurements:

> Which way of putting a product editor into a node of a zoomable React Flow board
> (`@xyflow/react` 12.12.1) is pointer-exact and usable, per engine, and what does each way cost?

A board is a zoomable canvas that shows model files of the repository next to text notes, images,
groups and arrows. A model on the board is edited in place with the product's own editor (bpmn-js,
dmn-js, the five Miragon renderers, all on one diagram-js) and stays in its own file. The board file
holds references, geometry, text and arrows, and never model content.

## What it is not

- No product feature. Nothing here is imported by the product, and neither page is a build input.
  Tailwind scans these files, this README and the test scripts too, so `vite build` emits the same
  files with and without the spike only while no word in them is a utility class name. Two mirror
  builds check it: byte-identical (275 files) after the build stages and again on the final state.
  In between, three words in four files had added three rules to the product's two style sheets
  (section 8). Round 5 ran the check again after every stage (identical, 275 files, on its final
  state), after one more such word in a test file was reworded. Round 6 ran it after each of its
  three stages and on its final state: identical, 275 files.
- No backend, no sign-in, no Live Host. The "repository" is a set of sample files in memory
  (`samples.ts`), and every change is gone with a reload.
- One person, one browser tab. Sync between two editors of one file runs through an in-page hub
  (`model-hub.ts`) that stands in for the product's live room.
- Measured in Chromium only (158, headless, with the sandbox, device pixel ratio 1 unless said).
- Not a design proposal for the board's look. It follows the design system so that the
  screenshots can be read, no more.

## Start the page

From the repository root, with no other service running:

```sh
pnpm --filter @designiq/web exec vite --port 5391 --strictPort
```

Then open `http://localhost:5391/board-spike.html`. Use the host `localhost`: the dev server
listens on `::1`, and `127.0.0.1` is refused. On the very first load of a cold dependency cache
Vite may reload the page once (the page is no build input, so its dependencies are found late).

| URL                                                 | Board                                                                                                                                                 |
| --------------------------------------------------- | ----------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------- |
| `/board-spike.html` or `?board=demo`                | The workshop board to look at: five models, a group, two notes, an image, drawn arrows and the derived ones. Add `&strategy=<s>` (default `overlay`). |
| `?board=compare`                                    | One BPMN file in six nodes, one per strategy (`c-<strategy>`). An edit in one shows in the other five.                                                |
| `?board=matrix&strategy=<s>&notations=bpmn,dmn`     | One node per notation (default: all seven), all in one strategy. The board the measurements run on. Node ids `m-<notation>`.                          |
| `?board=stress&strategy=<s>&notation=<n>&count=<k>` | `k` nodes of one file, for cost measurements. Node ids `s-0`, `s-1`, ...                                                                              |

`/board-spike-kit.html` is no board to look at: it runs the conformance kit of the modeler suite's
contract against the board module (section 15); `node apps/web/test/spike/contract-kit-chromium.ts`
opens it and prints the outcome.

Further parameters: `&zoom=<z>&x=<px>&y=<px>` set the first viewport (without them the board is
fitted into the window), `&arm=<nodeId>` arms a node on load (through its arm rules, after the
first fit), `&via=registry` makes every embed frame mount through the SPA's plugin registry,
`&mount=product` makes every `overlay` node mount the product's own editor instead of the spike's
reduced one (section 14).
Strategies: `naive`, `counter`, `overlay`, `iframe`, `plugin`, `shim`. Notations: `bpmn`, `dmn`,
`wardley`, `team-topology`, `event-storming`, `context-map`, `value-chain`.

The arm rules (built in round 5, section 12; two fixes and the final default in round 6, section
13). Each is a URL parameter with a default per strategy of the NODE; a parameter given in the URL
applies to every strategy.

| Parameter         | Values                    | Default for an `overlay` node      | Default for any other strategy | Meaning                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                   |
| ----------------- | ------------------------- | ---------------------------------- | ------------------------------ | --------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------- |
| `&armzoom=`       | `off`, `min1`, `exact1`   | `exact1` (round 5: `min1`)         | `off`                          | The board zoom a node is armed at. `off`: the board stays as it is. `min1`: a zoom up to 1.01 becomes exactly 1, a larger one stays. `exact1`: exactly 1 from anywhere. The board moves first, then the node is armed. The zoom changes about the pointer (a double-click) or about the node's centre (Edit, the keyboard, the hook, the URL); a node that fits is brought into the board's box below the toolbar with a 24 px margin, a longer one as "Behaviour that is a decision, not a result" says. |
| `&armedzoom=`     | `free`, `disarm`, `lock`  | `disarm`                           | `free`                         | What a zoom of the board does while a node is armed. `free`: it zooms under the armed editor. `disarm`: the node is disarmed first, then the zoom runs. `lock`: the zoom is refused and the toolbar's four zoom buttons are disabled. A pan never disarms.                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                |
| `&armreturn=`     | `stay`, `back`            | `stay`                             | `stay`                         | `back`: Done (the header's button or the toolbar's control), a click on the bare board and the hook's `arm(null)` bring the board back to the viewport it had before the arming moved it.                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                 |
| `&armmotion=<ms>` | a number of ms, 0 or more | 250 (the token `--cd-motion-base`) | the same                       | How long the board takes to move. Without the parameter: the token, and 0 under `prefers-reduced-motion`. The parameter overrides both.                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                   |

What a person sees on the default page (the demo board in `overlay`, which a 1100 x 650 window fits
at zoom 0.27):

- A double-click on a model node moves the board first, in about 250 ms, to 100 % about the
  double-clicked point, from any zoom, below 1 and above it. Only then the editor takes input. A
  model is edited at board zoom exactly 1; detail zoom is the editor's own zoom.
- A node that fits the board's box is brought into it. A node longer than the box (four of the
  demo's five models are 860 or 940 px wide, the board in that window 760 px) is moved only as far
  as leaves no gap, so the double-clicked point stays wherever no gap would open. The Edit button
  and the keyboard put such a node's top left corner at the margin instead.
- The toolbar's band has a Done control at its left end (`[data-board-done]`): always there,
  switched off while no model is edited, in reach wherever the armed node lies. The header's Edit
  and Done button is unchanged.
- A wheel zoom on the board or a toolbar zoom button makes the node a preview again before the
  board zooms.

| Added to the URL              | Board                                                                                                                                                    |
| ----------------------------- | -------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------- |
| nothing                       | The final default of an `overlay` node: `exact1`, `disarm`, `stay` (section 13)                                                                          |
| `&armzoom=min1`               | Round 5's default (`min1`, `disarm`, `stay`): above zoom 1 a node is armed at the zoom the board has. The board section 12 was measured on               |
| `&armzoom=off&armedzoom=free` | The board without rules, as in rounds 1 to 4: arming moves nothing, a zoom of the board runs under the armed editor. The board of sections 1 to 7 and 10 |

| Parameter                    | For    | Meaning                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                     |
| ---------------------------- | ------ | ------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------- |
| `&plugin=<comma list>`       | plugin | Layers of the outer-scale module: `drag`, `dragsvc` (the same site by replacing the service), `pan`, `wheel`, `pad`, `popup`, `tip`, `bend`, `edit`, `table`, `own`. Default: all but `dragsvc` and `own`. `plugin=none` is the bare engine, `plugin=all,own` adds `own`. The list in effect is on the engine host as `data-plugin-layers`. |
| `&shim=<comma list>`         | shim   | Parts: `events`, `rects`, `points`, `ctm`, `viewport`. Modifiers inside `all`: `snap`, `whole`, `css`, `clip`, `docrect`. Outside `all`: `guess`, `keep`. Absent or `all`: everything inside `all`. `shim=none` is the bare engine. `window.__boardSpikeShim` says what is patched right now.                                               |
| `&shimread=job` `tick` `arm` | shim   | How long a reading of the virtual space is trusted: per synchronous job (default), until the next viewport change, while armed. The last two measure what a stale reading costs.                                                                                                                                                            |

On the page: a double-click on a model node or its Edit button arms it (exactly one node is
armed). Done in the node's header, the Done control at the toolbar's left end, a click on the bare
board or arming another node disarms, and so does a zoom of the board under `armedzoom=disarm`.
The header moves an armed node. The toolbar changes board and strategy (the page reloads), zooms,
and adds notes, images, groups and models. The Files panel at the right shows every file as it is
right now and marks the lines the last change wrote.

## The six strategies

| Strategy  | What the armed editor is                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                               |
| --------- | ------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------ |
| `naive`   | The control. The editor's host stays in the node and is scaled by the board's CSS transform. Nothing compensates.                                                                                                                                                                                                                                      |
| `counter` | The host in the node is laid out at node size times board zoom and scaled back by `1 / zoom`, so its net scale is 1. The diagram's view is applied again on every board zoom step. diagram-js's popup menu and palette tooltip are created in a body-level host.                                                                                       |
| `overlay` | The same host element is moved into a screen-space layer above the board, placed with left, top, width and height. No ancestor of the editor carries a transform.                                                                                                                                                                                      |
| `iframe`  | The node body is a same-origin frame that shows `board-spike-embed.html`, which mounts one product editor with the product's own, unmodified mount function.                                                                                                                                                                                           |
| `plugin`  | The host stays in the node, scaled by the board, like `naive`. Every editor is constructed with the outer-scale module (`engines/outer-scale/`), one didi module that measures the host's net scale itself at every use and corrects the places where diagram-js mixes screen px with container px. No view is applied again on a board zoom.          |
| `shim`    | The host stays in the node, scaled by the board, like `naive`. While the node is armed, a page-level shim (`engines/coordinate-shim/`) gives the editor a virtual, unscaled coordinate space: 13 browser functions and getters answer in it and the editor's pointer events carry virtual coordinates. Nothing is patched while no shim node is armed. |

Not armed, a node is a preview: the editor is mounted from the start, takes no input, and its
editing chrome is hidden. The bpmn.io mark stays visible in every state.

## Run the checks

All of them are run by hand. None is part of `pnpm test` or of CI.

```sh
# pure modules (hub, board document, derived arrows, board actions, the board file under Git and two peers, the arm rules): 81 tests
node --test apps/web/test/spike/*.test.ts

# the smoke run: six strategies x seven notations x board zoom 0.5, 1 and 1.5 (126 cases)
node apps/web/test/spike/smoke-chromium.ts

# a battery on all six strategies, with a port, a cache and a result folder of its own
PORT=5421 CACHE_DIR=<scratch>/vite-cache OUT_DIR=<scratch>/out \
  STRATEGIES=naive,counter,overlay,iframe,plugin,shim JOBS=4 node apps/web/test/spike/bpmn-gestures-chromium.ts

# a variant of a strategy, with a script that knows nothing of it
EXTRA_PARAMS=plugin=all,own STRATEGIES=plugin CONCURRENCY=4 node apps/web/test/spike/miragon-b-chromium.ts

# a battery on `overlay` under the final default, under round 5's default, and on the board of round 4
STRATEGIES=overlay JOBS=4 node apps/web/test/spike/bpmn-chrome-chromium.ts
EXTRA_PARAMS='armzoom=min1' STRATEGIES=overlay JOBS=4 node apps/web/test/spike/bpmn-chrome-chromium.ts
EXTRA_PARAMS='armzoom=off&armedzoom=free' STRATEGIES=overlay JOBS=4 node apps/web/test/spike/bpmn-chrome-chromium.ts

# the arm rules themselves: 15 cases, 906 rows (`CASES=list` prints the row ids and runs nothing)
PORT=5451 CACHE_DIR=<scratch>/vite-cache OUT_DIR=<scratch>/run JOBS=4 node apps/web/test/spike/arm-rules-chromium.ts
```

Every script starts a Vite dev server of its own (in process) and a headless Chromium with the
sandbox, and opens a fresh page per row. It needs Playwright's Chromium headless shell
(`pnpm --filter @designiq/web exec playwright install --only-shell chromium`, once, when it is
missing). Do not run the product's `test:browser` or its `test/*-chromium.ts` scripts for this
spike: they launch Chromium without the sandbox. Each script's header lists its cases and variables.

| Script (`apps/web/test/spike/`)           | What it measures                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                        | Rows of the final run          | Run it on all six strategies                                                                                                                                                         |
| ----------------------------------------- | ----------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------- | ------------------------------ | ------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------ |
| `smoke-chromium.ts`                       | One shape drag per strategy, notation and zoom; travel and expectation from the zoom in effect (`zoomInEffect` per row)                                                                                                                                                 | 126                            | Default                                                                                                                                                                              |
| `bpmn-gestures-chromium.ts`               | 27 drag gestures of bpmn-js                                                                                                                                                                                                                                             | 810                            | `STRATEGIES=<six> JOBS=4`                                                                                                                                                            |
| `bpmn-chrome-chromium.ts`                 | 36 cases of bpmn-js that are no drag: pad, popups, palette, label editor, keys, wheel, focus                                                                                                                                                                            | 1092                           | `STRATEGIES=<six> JOBS=4`                                                                                                                                                            |
| `dmn-chromium.ts`                         | 25 cases: DRD, decision table, literal expression, simulation form                                                                                                                                                                                                      | 750 as built                   | `STRATEGIES=<six> ZOOMS=0.5,0.73,1,1.5,2 VARIANTS=as-built PARALLEL=4`                                                                                                               |
| `miragon-a-chromium.ts`                   | 41 cases on Wardley Map, Value Chain, Team Topology (40 of round 4 and board-zoom, round 6)                                                                                                                                                                             | 2492                           | `STRATEGIES=<six> CONCURRENCY=4`, and `ZOOMS=2 STRATEGIES=overlay,iframe,plugin,shim`                                                                                                |
| `miragon-b-chromium.ts`                   | 38 cases on Event Storming, Context Map (35 of round 4 and three board-zoom cases, round 6)                                                                                                                                                                             | 1568                           | The same two runs, `CONCURRENCY=4`                                                                                                                                                   |
| `sync-view-chromium.ts`                   | 18 cases: the view under board zoom, pan, node move and resize, arming, zoom extremes, two peers                                                                                                                                                                        | 984                            | `STRATEGIES=<six> CONCURRENCY=4`                                                                                                                                                     |
| `arm-rules-chromium.ts`                   | 15 cases (R1 to R15) on the arm rules: arming from a start zoom, preview against armed picture, a node larger than the board with the toolbar's Done control pressed, a board zoom while armed, interrupted moves, the way back, cost, what `exact1` costs above zoom 1 | 906 (round 6, two runs)        | Default is `overlay`; R9 always runs `iframe` and `plugin`. `CASES=R4,R14` (case tags or id prefixes), `REPEAT`, `JOBS` (at most 4). Rows that take times run alone after the others |
| `board-chromium.ts`, `board-file.test.ts` | The board itself: what a gesture writes, images, actions, derived arrows, gestures beside an armed editor, peers, size, Git merges                                                                                                                                      | 147 browser rows, 28 pure rows | Default (`overlay`, `plugin`, `iframe` for the parts `derived` and `armed`; `STRATEGIES` changes that)                                                                               |
| `cost-chromium.ts`                        | Mount time, memory, arming, frames, pointer moves, board work, idle (M1 to M7)                                                                                                                                                                                          | 585 samples per run            | Default. Needs a production build of the two pages and `BASE` (its header says how), one page at a time                                                                              |
| `plugin-chromium.ts`                      | The plugin builder's own matrix: `plugin` against `naive` and `overlay`, layer by layer                                                                                                                                                                                 | 1330 (main matrix)             | `PLUGIN="none;drag;all;all,own"` chooses layer lists                                                                                                                                 |
| `shim-chromium.ts`                        | The shim builder's own matrix: `shim` against `naive` and `overlay`, part by part (`CASES=list`)                                                                                                                                                                        | 1217                           | `SHIM` chooses part lists                                                                                                                                                            |

`<six>` is `naive,counter,overlay,iframe,plugin,shim`. The batteries' default is the first four.
On a 12 core machine under load, with 4 pages side by side, one battery on six strategies took 25
to 48 minutes. The row counts above are round 4's, on six strategies. Round 5 ran the six editor
batteries on `overlay` alone (135, 182, 125, 445, 280 and 178 rows, the last with 14 rows of a new
variant `burst`; 2 to 11 minutes each under load) and `arm-rules-chromium.ts` (871 s and 874 s with
`JOBS=4`). Round 6 ran them again on `overlay` under the final default: 135, 182, 125, 460, 295 and
178 rows (15 new rows each in `miragon-a` and `miragon-b`), 237 to 690 s per run with 4 pages side
by side at a 1 minute load of 18 to 49, and `arm-rules-chromium.ts` with 906 rows in 1419 s and
1415 s (two runs side by side, `JOBS=4`).

With the arm rules on, arming moves the board: the board zoom a script asks for (`ZOOMS`, `zoom:`)
is no longer the zoom the armed editor runs at. Since round 6 that holds above zoom 1 too: under
the default every `overlay` row runs at zoom 1, whatever was asked. Every battery row therefore
carries both, the zoom asked for (`zoom`, in `arm-rules`: `zoomAsked`) and the zoom in effect after
arming (`zoomInEffect`; `effect.zoom` in `bpmn-gestures`, `env.zoom` in `bpmn-chrome`). Tables and
row ids stay keyed by the zoom asked for.

A verdict rule that judges a zoom of the board reads the node's policy first (`board.armPolicy(id)`)
since round 6: `bpmn-chrome` wheel-unarmed and wheel-limits, `dmn` switch-views-zoomed, `sync-view`
zoom-label, zoom-popup, pan by wheel and zoom-plain by toolbar, and the board-zoom cases of
`miragon-a` and `miragon-b`. Under `disarm` a node that is a preview before the first zoom tick is
ok, and a late disarm or a zoom tick under an armed node is broken; under `free` the rule of round
4 stands, so the control run stays comparable. `armedzoom=lock` has a branch that never ran in the
two Miragon scripts and none in `bpmn-chrome`, which would judge it by the rule for `free`.
`bpmn-gestures` reports a resize handle that never appears as unmeasured, not as broken.

| Variable       | Meaning                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                |
| -------------- | ---------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------- |
| `PORT`         | Port of the script's own dev server (default 5391). A taken port fails at once.                                                                                                                                                                                                                                        |
| `BASE`         | Use a running dev server instead, for example `http://localhost:5391`. None is started then, and a dependency that server finds late is not noticed (the reload itself still fails the run).                                                                                                                           |
| `CACHE_DIR`    | Vite's dependency cache for that server (default `apps/web/node_modules/.vite-board-spike-<PORT>`).                                                                                                                                                                                                                    |
| `OUT_DIR`      | Folder for results and screenshots. Never inside the repository (see `outDir()` in `lib.ts`).                                                                                                                                                                                                                          |
| `OUT`          | The result file of a script that writes exactly one (the smoke run: `<OUT_DIR>/smoke.json`).                                                                                                                                                                                                                           |
| `EXTRA_PARAMS` | A query string, for example `plugin=all,own`, `shim=all,guess` or, two parameters joined by an ampersand, `armzoom=off&armedzoom=free` (the arm rules off: the board of round 4), appended to every board URL the harness opens. A parameter the script sets itself wins. The result file carries it as `extraParams`. |

The exit code is 1 when the harness failed (wrong page, an editor that did not mount, a
navigation under the run, a console error, a timeout). What a measurement says about a strategy
is in the result, never in the exit code.

The static gates on the spike's files (plain `pnpm arch` answers from a cache that does not hold
untracked files, so dependency-cruiser is run without it):

```sh
pnpm exec prettier --check apps/web/src/board-spike apps/web/test/spike apps/web/board-spike.html apps/web/board-spike-embed.html
pnpm exec eslint apps/web/src/board-spike apps/web/test/spike
pnpm --filter @designiq/web typecheck
pnpm exec depcruise apps/web/src/board-spike --no-cache --config .dependency-cruiser.mjs
```

### Writing a further measurement

`apps/web/test/spike/lib.ts` is the harness every such script shares: server, browser, a board by
its URL parameters, one handle per node that works the same in all six strategies and answers in
top-level screen coordinates, pointer and key input, console and page errors, screenshots, CDP
metrics and the result file. Its header and the comments on `Board` and `NodeHandle` are the
reference. The shortest useful script:

```ts
import { centre, run, writeResult } from "./lib.ts";

await run(async (harness) => {
  const board = await harness.open({
    board: "matrix",
    strategy: "overlay",
    notations: ["bpmn"],
    zoom: 0.73,
    x: 60,
    y: 80,
  });
  await board.arm("m-bpmn");
  const node = await board.node("m-bpmn");
  const before = (await node.elementBox("Task_check_credit"))!;
  const dragged = await node.drag(centre(before), { x: 44, y: 29 }, { hold: ["Control"] });
  const after = (await node.elementBox("Task_check_credit"))!;
  const ratio = (after.x - before.x) / dragged.delta.x;
  console.log(writeResult("example", { ratio, netScale: await node.netScale(), problems: await board.problems() }));
  await board.close();
});
```

Traps that cost runs before they were known:

- Under `shim`, while a node is armed, the page's plain geometry functions answer in the editor's
  virtual space. `lib.ts` and the probe read the browser's own functions (`window.__boardSpikeNative`,
  `native-geometry.ts`). A geometry read in a script's own `page.evaluate` must do the same.
- A pad or popup read at once is not placed yet, and the product's theme lets it pop in over 150 ms.
  `board.waitForBox()` waits for the finite, box-moving animations first; `board.animationsRest()`
  does the same for any other read.
- diagram-js swallows a click for 400 ms after a drag ends. Wait 450 ms before the next click.
- Hit-test before every press (`node.hit(point)`), and compare a displacement with the travel the
  harness reports (`dragged.delta`), never with the travel asked for.
- The browser has one clipboard for all pages of a run: pages that copy and paste side by side read
  each other's content. Serialise every copy-to-paste span.
- A diagram-js listener that returns anything but `undefined` stops the event.
- With the arm rules on (the default of an `overlay` node), `board.arm()` moves the board and
  resolves after the move. Read `board.viewport()` after arming and compute every expectation from
  it: a pointer travel is flow units times the zoom in effect, and the node is where the rule put
  it. Under the default, in a 2200 x 1300 window, a node at (60, 80) armed by the hook ends at
  (24, 79) from any zoom below 1, stays at (60, 80) from zoom 1 (no transition at all), and ends
  at (260, 215) from 1.5 and at (460, 350) from 2, always at zoom 1. Each of the six editor
  batteries had at least one rule that used the zoom asked for or the URL's node position; all
  are fixed (section 12).
- Where the node ends depends on what armed it. `board.armByDoubleClick()` and any real
  double-click are anchored at the pointer: a node longer than the board's box is moved only to
  close a gap. `board.arm()`, `arm=`, the Edit button and the keyboard are anchored at the node's
  centre: such a node starts at the margin. `(await board.lastTransition()).anchor` says which. To
  see a double-click move nothing, the node must cover the box on BOTH axes before it: a shape in
  the node's lower right placed at the board's middle leaves a gap at zoom 2.5 to 3.5.
- The toolbar's band is part of the placement: 55 px in a 2200 x 1300 window, 95 px at 1280 x 720
  (55 before round 6), 1100 x 650 and 1000 x 600, 135 px at 800 x 500, 175 px at 640 x 360 and
  500 x 400. A hook arming at 1280 x 720 ends at (24, 119), not at (24, 79).
- The way out of an armed node that is always in reach: `board.pressDone()`, a real click on the
  toolbar's Done control (`pressed: false` means nothing was clicked). `board.doneControl()` reads
  its box, `enabled`, `nodeId`, `takesPress` and the band. The control is in the document while it
  is off: test `enabled` or `nodeId`, never existence. A press on the switched-off control still
  dispatches a pointerdown and interrupts a running move.
- Chromium sends no mouseup for a release over a disabled button. Until the fix after round 6's
  batteries a drag that ended over a switched-off toolbar button was never ended (section 13); the
  toolbar's switched-off buttons take no pointer events now, any other disabled button still does it.
- `node.select()` clicks a shape's middle only. `node.pressPoint(id)` searches a 5 x 5 grid of the
  shape's box for a point that takes the press, `node.selectAnywhere(id)` clicks it.
- A second click on a selected pool or lane takes the selection away in bpmn-js (4 of 4 pages).
  To bring a resize handle that did not appear, select the shape anew.
- A double-click does not wait for the move: use `board.armByDoubleClick()` or
  `board.waitForRest()`. A box read while the board moves is a moving box, and a press sent during
  the move lands on whatever the moving board puts under it.
- Under `armedzoom=disarm` every `board.setViewport()` with another zoom disarms the node, and
  `board.showNode()` calls it twice. With the current zoom it is a pan and safe.
- A case about a zoom gesture sends `board.wheelBurst()` (several events a frame apart): a single
  wheel event of the harness has no inertia. Playwright's wheel event reports no pressed button
  even while one is down.
- The frame sampler (`board.startFrames()`, `stopFrames()`) runs before the move's own step, so a
  sample shows the state after the frame before. The last eased frames are at zoom 0.9999 and
  more: compare with exactly 1, never with a rounded value.
- `counters().arm.transitions` is 0 when the node was in place already: read
  `board.lastTransition()` by its id. Keys pressed while the board moves are lost.
- A fixed pixel travel is a trap below zoom 1 and after an earlier move: bpmn-js refuses a drop of
  130 units to the right of the sample's task (ratio 0, file unchanged, 3 of 3) whatever the rules.
- A point built by adding a float step many times falls on either side of a whole px depending on
  the node's place in the window (540.9999999999998 against 676.0000000000003): compute an aim
  point as an offset from the host's corner and round it before aiming. Compare two end viewports
  with a tolerance, not with `===` (11 of 36 differed in the 13th decimal).
- Rows that follow the machine's load: a palette drag that starts within 20 px of the canvas edge
  auto-scrolls when the pointer's steps are slow (four create rows of `arm-rules` at a load of 40
  to 48), and the pointer's place after an auto-scroll with a shape held outside the editor (4 to
  6 rows per run). Run time-taking and edge rows alone.
- The decision table's cells lie 400 px and more below the node's top: at zoom 4 with the node at
  (60, 80) none is in the window.

## Map of the files

Since section 17 this folder is the measuring bench alone. The board's own files moved to
`src/notations/board/` under the same names, with four exceptions: `strategies/overlay-product.tsx`
is `overlay-surface.tsx` there, `strategies/types.ts` is `surface.ts`, `embed/product-mount.ts` and
`embed/inpage-contract.ts` lost their folder, and `module/` is `contract/` (its `kit-main.ts`
stayed here). The map below is the layout of the measuring rounds.

| Path                                                                                            | What it is                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                             |
| ----------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------- | ------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------ |
| `apps/web/board-spike.html`                                                                     | The board page. Dev only: served by the Vite dev server, listed in no build input.                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                     |
| `apps/web/board-spike-embed.html`                                                               | The page an iframe node shows. Dev only.                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                               |
| `shared.ts`                                                                                     | The contract: strategies, notations, `window.__boardSpike`, `window.__boardSpikeEmbed`, `NativeGeometry`, the probe, the frame protocol. Plain TypeScript Node can load.                                                                                                                                                                               |
| `native-geometry.ts`                                                                            | The browser's own geometry functions, captured before any strategy code runs and published as `window.__boardSpikeNative`. First import of both entries.                                                                                                                                                                                               |
| `main.tsx`, `page-styles.ts`                                                                    | Entry of the board page and the stylesheets both pages need.                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                           |
| `samples.ts`                                                                                    | The sample files of all seven notations, the image, and the four boards.                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                               |
| `model-hub.ts`                                                                                  | One server document per file, one replica per bound editor, the bridge to embed frames over `postMessage`.                                                                                                                                                                                                                                             |
| `board-doc.ts`, `board-actions.ts`                                                              | The board as a structured live document and everything that writes into it, one transaction per finished gesture.                                                                                                                                                                                                                                      |
| `board-flow.tsx`, `board.tsx`                                                                   | The board document in React Flow's controlled mode, and the `<ReactFlow>` shell with a lazy slot per part and the guards that keep the board out of an armed editor's clicks.                                                                                                                                                                          |

Plain TypeScript Node can load.                                         |
| `board-arming.ts`                                                                               | The arm rules at work, the same for every strategy: the board's own move per animation frame, what interrupts it, the kind of anchor of an arming (fixed when it begins, written into every transition record), the guards that see a zoom coming (wheel, pinch, toolbar) or meet it in its first tick, the way back, counters and transition records. |
| `board-gestures.tsx`, `toolbar.tsx`                                                             | Paste and drop of images, arrows dropped on a node, which group takes a dragged node; the toolbar, with the Done control of the armed model (`[data-board-done]`, round 6).                                                                                                                                                                            |

Drawn dashed, stored nowhere.                                                                                                                                                                                                                                             |

| `strategies/`                                                                                   | One surface per strategy: `naive.tsx`, `counter.tsx`, `overlay.tsx`, `iframe.tsx`, `plugin.tsx`, `shim.tsx` with `shim.css`; `index.ts` registers them, `types.ts` is their contract.                                                                                                                                                                  |
