Paths without a folder are in `apps/web/src/board-spike/`.

## Behaviour that is a decision, not a result

- Deleting a group removes its members from the board (the model files stay). A member that is
  armed at that moment stays, at its place, as a node of the board itself.
- A note's Markdown is a small pipeline of its own, not the product's Markdown view: no images, no
  model links, a line break in the text is a line break in the note.
- Image types and the 10 MiB limit are those of the Live Host's image route.
- A node belongs to the group its centre lies in when its drag ends. Groups do not nest.
- An armed node is no tab stop and its wrapper takes no focus. Whether an armed node should be
  reachable by Tab is not decided here.
- The armed node cannot be deleted. After a press inside the armed editor, Backspace and Delete do
  not delete another selected board node (since round 5 in `overlay` too: a press in the overlay
  layer counts as a press in the armed editor); the toolbar's delete button does.

The arm rules rest on the following decisions of the build stage of round 5 and of the fix stage
of round 6 (the default, the kind of anchor, a node larger than the box, the Done control, the zoom
label). A product has to make each of them too.

| Matter                                     | As built                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                       |
| ------------------------------------------ | ------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------ |
| Which zoom counts as 1                     | Tolerance 0.01: under `min1` any zoom up to 1.01 becomes exactly 1 (a fit lands on 0.9973 or 1.0004; the smallest step made on purpose is 1.149 by wheel and 1.2 by the toolbar). 1.011 and 1.02 stay.                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                         |
| Above zoom 1                               | `exact1` (the default of `overlay` since round 6) brings the board down to exactly 1: a model is edited at board zoom 1, the one state every engine is built for, and detail zoom is the editor's own. `min1` (round 5's default) arms at the zoom the board has. Section 13 has the numbers for the default, section 12 for `min1`.                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                           |
| Where the node ends up                     | The board's box below the toolbar's whole band, minus 24 px. The zoom is applied about an anchor (the pointer for a double-click; the node's centre for Edit, the keyboard, the hook and the URL), then the board is shifted by the least amount that puts the whole wrapper into that box. A changed target is whole px; a node within 0.5 px of the box counts as in.                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                        |
| The kind of anchor                         | Decided once, when the arming begins: a request with a pointer (the double-click) is anchored at the pointer, any other at the node's centre. The Edit button and the keyboard count as no pointer: a click on Edit is no place in the diagram. The look after the last write and a request that joins a running move take the arming's kind. Every move records it (`transition.anchor`).                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                     |
| A node larger than that box                | Per axis on which the wrapper is longer than the box minus the margin. Centre: its start goes to the margin, so the header's left end and the palette are in reach. Pointer: the least shift that leaves no gap (a start beyond the low margin goes to it, an end short of the high margin goes to it), and a wrapper that covers the box is not moved, so the double-clicked point stays. The zoom is never lowered to make a node fit. Round 5 always put the start at the margin. The price of the pointer rule is measured in section 13: header and palette can lie outside the board, and a node that leaves a gap still moves by that gap.                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                              |
| A narrow board                             | The toolbar wraps and its band grows. With the Done control the toolbar is 1050 px wide in one row (830 to 835 px before round 6), which needs a board of 1080 px (a window of 1420 px with the Files panel open). Band: 55 px on a 1860 px wide board, 95 px at 660, 760 and 940 px (the 1280 x 720 window, 55 px before round 6), 135 px in an 800 x 500 window, 175 px at 640 x 360 and 500 x 400. The box is read when arming; a resize while armed re-places nothing.                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                     |
| The way out: a Done control in the toolbar | Round 6. A button at the toolbar's first place on screen and its last in the document: one Shift + Tab from an armed `overlay` editor (12 stops were measured with it first in the document), so focus order and visual order differ inside the toolbar. ALWAYS present, and switched off (`disabled`, 50 % opacity) while no model node is armed, while the rules move the board and while a text note is edited: the toolbar wraps the same way armed and unarmed, so the band a placement is computed with is the band the armed node finds, and nothing moves under the pointer. ONE box of 200 x 28 px (130 px in a window up to 760 px wide), the model's name cut by an ellipsis, the whole name in title and label. It makes the call the header's Done makes; the header's button is unchanged. `disabled` and not `aria-disabled`: after Done by the keyboard the focus falls to body, and a drag released over the switched-off button is never ended (section 13). |
| The toolbar's zoom label                   | One width (52 px at least, digits of one width): a label as wide as its text made the toolbar wrap on arming at 5 of 15 window widths, and one arming then made two transitions.                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                               |
| The move                                   | The board moves first, the node is a preview until the last write and is armed in the same task. Duration `--cd-motion-base` (250 ms), easing `--cd-ease`, 0 under reduced motion; linear in x, y and zoom. Not React Flow's animated `setViewport` (its promise never settles once it is interrupted).                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                        |
| What interrupts a move                     | A wheel on the board, any press inside the board (the toolbar included), Escape, arming another node, a disarm, a toolbar zoom, any other viewport change. The board then stays where it is and nothing is armed. A wheel or press outside the board (Files panel) does not.                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                   |
| Keys during a move                         | Escape stops it. Every other key is swallowed and lost, not queued (modifier keys and keys in a text field pass): the double-click has selected the node, and Backspace would delete it.                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                       |
| The same node asked for twice              | A second double-click: its first press stops the move, the double-click starts a new one from there (2 transitions). The hook asking twice joins the running move (1 transition). A double-click that lands on the header's Edit button is two Edit clicks: 2 transitions.                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                     |
| A window resize during a move              | Does not interrupt. The target is computed again after the last write, with the arming's kind of anchor, at most 3 moves per arming. After an arming without a pointer the node is placed again ((24, 79), then (24, 119)); after a pointer arming only a gap is closed, so a node that covers the smaller box stays where it is.                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                              |
| What counts as a zoom                      | A wheel that cannot change the zoom (no vertical part, or pushing against 0.1 or 4), 100 % at zoom 1 and a step at the range's end are left alone. Fit always counts. A pan never disarms.                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                     |
| The armed node's header                    | Is the board: a wheel there disarms and zooms.                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                 |
| A press held in the armed editor           | Under `disarm` a zoom is then refused instead of disarming, so a held drag survives. A zoom not seen coming (a script's `setViewport`) is then undone inside its tick, as under `lock`.                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                        |
| A wheel inside the armed editor            | Is the editor's, under every policy. It never reaches the board.                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                               |
| What a zoom disarm keeps                   | Typed text of an open label editor is committed as by a click on the bare board. A popup closes, the selection and a pending palette tool are gone. The board stays at the zoom the gesture ends at.                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                           |
| Arming B while A is armed                  | A becomes a preview before the first write; with no move needed A is swapped to B as before the rules.                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                         |
| `armreturn=back`                           | Done (either control), a click on the bare board and the hook's `arm(null)` go back to the viewport before the arming, also after a pan while armed. A disarm by a zoom, by arming another node, by a text note or by Add text never goes back.                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                |
| `arm=` in the URL                          | Under `armzoom=off` armed from the first render; otherwise through the rule after the first fit and the editor's mount. The board's first viewport is not a zoom under an armed node.                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                          |

## Findings

Sources: the final run of each battery on the fixed board with all six strategies (round 4, run A),
a second run of `plugin` and `shim` (run B), the board battery (two runs), the cost run on a
production build (two runs), the two builders' own scripts, the build stages, and an independent
check of sixteen statements with scripts of its own (section 10). Chromium
158.0.8127.18 headless with the sandbox, window 2200 x 1300, device pixel ratio 1. Board zoom 0.5,
0.73, 1, 1.5 and 2 unless a row says otherwise. Times taken on a loaded machine are not cited.

Round 5 built the three arm rules the recommendation leans on and measured them. Its sources, all
in section 12: the builder's before and after runs, the battery `arm-rules` (723 rows, two runs),
the six editor batteries again on `overlay` (a control run with the rules off, two or three runs
with that round's default rules, `min1`, and supplements), and a second independent check of
fourteen claims.
Sections 1 to 7 and 10 describe the board with the rules off, which is the board of round 4:
with `armzoom=off&armedzoom=free` the six batteries on `overlay` agree with round 4 in the verdict
of 1326 of 1331 rows (135, 177 of 182, 125, 164, 445 and 280). The 5 others are `bpmn-chrome`'s
wheel-limits rows, degraded before and ok since the range clamp of round 5 (section 8).

Round 6, the closing round, changed two findings of round 5 (a node larger than the board, the
Done button out of reach), made `exact1` the default of an `overlay` node and measured the result.
Its sources, all in section 13: the fix stage's runs before and after the change (153 pages each)
and its acceptance run, the battery `arm-rules` (906 rows, two runs), the six editor batteries
again on `overlay` with policy-aware verdict rules (a control run with the rules off, two runs
under the final default, supplements), and a third independent check of ten claims (about 4,300
pages). Section 12 is the record of round 5, whose default was `min1`. With
`armzoom=off&armedzoom=free` the six batteries equal round 5's control run in the verdict of 1345
of 1345 rows (135, 182, 125, 445, 280 and 178).

### 1. The question and the answer

1. `naive` is exact only at board zoom 1 (27 of 27 bpmn-js gestures ok there). Away from 1 it wrote
   wrong values into the model file in 73 of 135 gesture rows. It is the control.
2. `counter` and `overlay` are pointer-exact for all seven engines (bpmn-js ratios 0.97 to 1.01, no
   wrong-data row in any engine) and equal each other in verdict in 135 of 135, 182 of 182, 125 of 125, 164 of 164, 356 of 356
   and 218 of 224 rows. Their price: chrome and HTML views keep their screen size (the decision table
   is 0 % visible at zoom 0.5, 14 of 25 DMN cases are broken there), and the view is applied again per
   zoom step (56.5 % out of scale at 39 of 40 steps while an editor is open).
3. `iframe` has no broken and no wrong-data row of its own in the six editor batteries. Its price: 3.1 times the
   JS heap per editor (1486 against 473 KB), about 3 times the mount time per editor (67.5 against
   19.6 to 21.9 ms, one run), popups confined to the frame (17 % of the replace menu cut in a
   640 x 388 node), and chrome that scales with the board (pad entries 11 px at zoom 0.5).
4. `plugin` makes bpmn-js and the DRD exact (135 of 135 gesture rows, 175 of 182 chrome rows ok). It
   does not reach code outside diagram-js: the decision table has 20 of 125 rows not ok, and with the
   default layers four sites of three Miragon renderers write wrong data. 20 renderer-own sites are
   wrong by default; the layer `own` repairs three kinds of them (draw tools, palette tooltips, the
   team band) by private name.
5. `shim` has no broken and no wrong-data row of its own in those batteries: bpmn-js 301 of 317 rows ok, DMN 124 of 125,
   Miragon 662 ok and 48 degraded of 725. Its price is not in the rows: 13 browser functions are
   patched page-wide while a node is armed, a caller rule answers wrong for editor code in a timer
   (0 of 13 right), it is measured in Chromium only, and one gap was found only by an independent
   battery (the FEEL autocomplete list is not shown in a low window).
6. Per engine: for bpmn-js and the DRD every way but `naive` is pointer-exact. For the decision table
   only `iframe` and `shim` have no broken row at any zoom. For the Miragon renderers `counter`,
   `overlay`, `iframe` and `shim` are exact, `plugin` only once the renderers fix their own math.
7. Cost: the five in-page strategies need the same memory (472 to 474 KB of heap per editor), no
   strategy's pointer move differs from another's by more than about 0.8 ms of main thread time, and
   nothing polls at rest (0 layouts, 0 style recalcs, 0 script in 72 of 72 idle samples).
8. At board zoom 1 no strategy has a broken or wrong-data row in five batteries (233 rows each):
   `naive` 208 ok and 22 degraded, `counter` 226 and 4, `overlay` 227 and 3, `iframe` 221 and 12,
   `plugin` 227 and 3, `shim` 228 and 2. For `naive` this holds for the batteries' node position
   only, (60, 80) px from the board's origin: its popups are off by the node's place on the screen,
   and with the host at x 1050 the replace menu opens 1050 px to the right, outside the window (0 of
   8 entries take a press, 3 of 3 rows, section 10).
9. Shared by all six: a peer's edit closes an open bpmn-js or DRD label editor and the typed text is
   lost (3 of 3 rows per strategy), and a label editor closed without typing changes the file at the
   node's fitted scale in every strategy but `plugin`.
10. The board file is fit for review (one changed line per touched element in 28 of 29 gestures,
    182 B per element) and not yet fit for merging: a plain Git 3-way merge conflicts on neighbouring
    lines (5 of 15 pairs), and 4 of 10 concurrent edits of two peers leave wrong data in the file.
11. Round 5, `overlay` with the arm rules as built (`min1`, `disarm`, `stay`; section 12): asked at
    board zoom 0.5 or 0.73, every one of 466 rows per run of the five editor batteries runs at
    zoom exactly 1, and 227 of 233 rows per asked zoom are ok (round 4: 191 and 211). No frame
    shows an armed editor at another board zoom than the one it was armed at. What the rules leave
    or add: above zoom 1 the armed decision table is not its preview (a cell jumps 129 to 1159 px),
    the Done button is out of reach when the node is wider than the board (5 of 8 rows), a node
    larger than the board's box is thrown to its top left corner, and the picture moves under the
    pointer when the node has to be shifted into the box (median 105 px).
12. Round 6, `overlay` under the final default (`exact1`, `disarm`, `stay`; section 13), which
    replaces the default of item 11: asked at board zoom 0.5 to 2, every one of 1167 rows per run
    of the five editor batteries runs at zoom exactly 1, and 228 of 233 rows per asked zoom are ok
    with none broken. An armed HTML view is its preview from above zoom 1 too (0 px in 36 of 36
    rows from 1.02 to 4), and a Done control in the toolbar ended the editing in 339 of 339 and 504
    of 504 real presses wherever the node lay. A node that covers the board's box is no longer
    moved by a double-click (0 px, 4 of 4). What stays or is new: a double-click on a node longer
    than the board's box from an overview leaves 0 of 14 palette entries in 45 of 51 rows, an
    arming above zoom 1 takes about 290 ms where it took 26 ms, and the decision table shows 41 %
    at every asked zoom. Found by the independent check and fixed after the batteries (section
    13): a drag released over a switched-off toolbar button was never ended (9 of 9 pages, 0 of 48
    after the fix).

### 2. Matrix

Cells are the batteries' verdicts with the board zooms they hold at. "except at 1" means at 0.5,
0.73, 1.5 and 2. A cell without a zoom holds at every measured zoom.

**bpmn-js** (`bpmn-gestures`, `bpmn-chrome`; node 800 x 508 unless said)

| What a person does                                                           | naive                                                       | counter                            | overlay                            | iframe                                                    | plugin                                      | shim                         |
| ---------------------------------------------------------------------------- | ----------------------------------------------------------- | ---------------------------------- | ---------------------------------- | --------------------------------------------------------- | ------------------------------------------- | ---------------------------- |
| Move a shape, a selection, an external label                                 | wrong data except at 1                                      | ok                                 | ok                                 | ok                                                        | ok                                          | ok                           |
| Create (palette drag and click, append, paste at the pointer)                | wrong data except at 1, append broken above 1               | ok, palette create degraded at 0.5 | ok, palette create degraded at 0.5 | ok                                                        | ok                                          | ok                           |
| Connect (pad, global tool), reconnect an end                                 | wrong data except at 1, reconnect broken except at 1        | ok                                 | ok                                 | ok                                                        | ok                                          | ok                           |
| Resize a pool, a lane                                                        | wrong data except at 1                                      | ok                                 | ok                                 | lane ok, pool degraded (canvas auto-scrolls, width right) | ok                                          | ok                           |
| Lasso, space tool                                                            | broken (lasso), wrong data (space tool) except at 1         | ok                                 | ok                                 | ok                                                        | ok                                          | ok                           |
| Move a bendpoint, a segment                                                  | broken except at 1                                          | ok                                 | ok                                 | ok                                                        | ok                                          | ok                           |
| Canvas pan, hand tool                                                        | degraded except at 1                                        | ok                                 | ok                                 | ok                                                        | ok                                          | ok                           |
| Drop into a lane or a sub-process, alignment guides, auto-scroll at the edge | wrong data except at 1                                      | ok                                 | ok                                 | ok                                                        | ok                                          | ok                           |
| Wheel zoom (Control + wheel): the point under the pointer                    | degraded except at 1                                        | ok                                 | ok                                 | ok                                                        | ok                                          | ok                           |
| Wheel scroll: distance per step                                              | degraded except at 1                                        | ok                                 | ok                                 | degraded except at 1                                      | ok                                          | degraded except at 1         |
| Wheel zoom at the editor's limit after a board zoom                          | ok                                                          | degraded                           | degraded                           | ok                                                        | ok                                          | ok                           |
| Context pad                                                                  | degraded below 1, broken above 1                            | degraded at 0.5, 0.73              | degraded at 0.5, 0.73              | degraded at 0.5; in a 640 x 388 node at every zoom        | degraded at 0.5                             | degraded at 0.5              |

Legend. `ok`: as a person expects. `degraded`: it works, at a cost the row names (off its anchor,
scrolled, cut, a target below 24 px, text below 9 px). `broken`: the gesture cannot be done or does
not do what the screen shows; the file holds nothing wrong. `wrong data`: the file holds a value or a
relation that contradicts what the person did. The batteries name `naive`'s error differently: a
value written off the pointer by the board zoom is wrong data in `bpmn-gestures`, broken in `dmn` and
`miragon-b`, degraded in `miragon-a`. The matrix says wrong data wherever the file holds that value
and names the script's word. The counts below are the scripts' own. The Value Chain space tool at
zoom 2 is degraded in the four strategies run there by a rule of the script and is left out.

The matrix and the counts are round 4's: the board with the arm rules off. One row has changed
since with the rules off: "Wheel zoom at the editor's limit after a board zoom" is ok in `counter`
and `overlay` since the range clamp of round 5 (section 8). What the arm rules change in
`overlay`'s cells is in section 12, and under the final default in section 13.

The 12 or 15 unmeasured `miragon-a` rows are the product's Keyboard shortcuts dialog, which only the
frame's product mount builds. Repeats: `plugin` and `shim` were run twice and agree in every verdict
(bpmn-gestures 270 of 270, bpmn-chrome 364 of 364 in every number, dmn 0 of 250 rows differ,
sync-view 328 of 328, miragon-a 709 of 712 rows identical, miragon-b 559 of 560). The other four ran
once on the fixed board; against the run before the fix, every verdict change is one of the fixed
board bugs (bpmn-gestures 20, bpmn-chrome 0, dmn 500 of 500 rows as expected, sync-view 20 of 21,
miragon 39) except one sync-view row of crossed writes in the frame.
