### 10. The independent check

Sixteen statements of this README went to a separate agent whose task was to refute them with
scripts of its own on `lib.ts`: about 1,200 rows, three repeats on fresh pages, "refuted" as the
default when in doubt. 13 hold as written, 3 hold with a correction, none is refuted. The repeats
agree in every measured number except one time and one `overlay` create row (0.35 to 0.53 px). The
corrections and the further findings are worked into the sections above.

| Statement                                            | Result                                                                                                                                                                           |
| ---------------------------------------------------- | -------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------- |
| `naive`: displacement is travel times the board zoom | Holds in all seven notations from the file: ratio 0.50 / 1.00 / 1.50 at zoom 0.5 / 1 / 1.5 (63 rows)                                                                             |
| The other five: a BPMN move and a palette create     | Hold: move ratio 0.995 to 1.003, create 0.33 to 1.87 px from the drop (120 rows)                                                                                                 |
| `naive` writes a wrong column width                  | Holds (150 at 0.5, 328 at 1.5). Corrected: a click on the column's edge writes a width in every strategy, only `naive` writes a wrong one (section 7)                            |
| `plugin` on the decision table                       | Holds (width 232). Corrected: at 0.5 the context menu is also 40 px off in x and cut to half its height, and the hit policy list has `naive`'s numbers (section 4)               |
| `shim` on the decision table                         | Holds: width 232, list at (0, 0) px, COLLECT written 6 of 6, caret right 6 of 6. The caret is right in `naive` too, so that row tells no strategy apart                          |
| `plugin` on a Context Map writes the team            | Holds, 3 of 3; `plugin=all,own`, `shim` and `overlay` write the name                                                                                                             |
| `plugin`: draw tools of Wardley and Event Storming   | Hold: 94 to 436 px off with the default layers, 0.77 to 1.44 px with `own`, 0.77 to 2.12 px in `shim`, 0.42 to 0.85 px in `overlay`                                              |
| `counter`, `overlay`: no table row in reach at 0.5   | Holds, and at 0.73 too (section 3)                                                                                                                                               |
| `iframe` cuts the replace menu in a 640 x 388 node   | Holds at five zooms: 85.7 % of it in reach, the cut part is the title and the search field, 8 of 8 entries take a press                                                          |
| A board zoom under an open editor or popup           | Holds: `plugin` and `shim` stay in scale and keep the popup, in `counter` and `overlay` the picture is 0.574 to 1.742 times the host's scale and the Wardley and DRD popup close |
| `shim` leaves nothing behind after disarming         | Holds in 12 of 12 cycles: 13 of 13 functions native again, 25 listeners on the window gone                                                                                       |
| A label editor closed without typing                 | Holds: `plugin` 0 of 27 closes write, every other strategy writes at the node's first fit                                                                                        |
| The four board fixes, as built                       | Hold: hit policy picked by pointer in `naive` at zoom 1, three shapes moved together in `counter`, the node armed after a release over the pane in 24 of 24 rows                 |
| Keys, wheel and selection stay with the armed editor | Hold in `plugin` and `shim`, 6 of 6 rows each, board file byte-identical                                                                                                         |
| Two nodes on one file                                | Hold: the move shows at the same model position in the other node, one mount per node                                                                                            |
| Popups at the board's edge                           | Corrected for `plugin` (cut by the board's box, not kept in the node) and `shim` (flipped and whole), section 3                                                                  |

Found beyond the statements: `shim` answers board code virtually for the rest of a job (section 3),
the Context Map name cannot be edited by pointer at all under `plugin` at zoom 1.5 and 2 (section
4), `counter` and `overlay` cannot open the decision table by pointer at 0.5 (section 3), and
`naive`'s popups at zoom 1 are off by the node's place on the screen (section 1). Four attempts to
break `plugin` and `shim` found nothing: arming node A, then B, then A again; moving the armed node by
its header before a shape drag; device pixel ratio 1.25 and 2; a shape move in all seven notations
at zoom 0.5, 1.5 and 2 (file ratio 0.981 to 1.016 and 0.988 to 1.015).

The check did not cover: times, touch and pen, any browser but headless Chromium, the variants of
`shim`, and layer lists of `plugin` other than the default and `all,own`.

### 11. Recommendation

This section is a judgment, not a measurement. It is the orchestrating agent's reading of sections
1 to 13, rewritten after the closing round. Every number in it is measured and comes from the
section named beside it.

Verdicts per asked board zoom in the five editor batteries (ok / degraded / broken / wrong data,
of 233 rows; `naive` and `counter` at zoom 2: 88 rows; up to 4 rows per cell could not be
measured). The first six columns are round 4: the board without arm rules, each editor armed at
the asked zoom. The last column is `overlay` under the final rules (round 6, section 13), where
every row runs at board zoom exactly 1 whatever zoom was asked for:

| Asked board zoom | naive             | counter           | overlay           | iframe           | plugin           | shim             | overlay, final rules |
| ---------------- | ----------------- | ----------------- | ----------------- | ---------------- | ---------------- | ---------------- | -------------------- |
| 0.5              | 78 / 93 / 33 / 26 | 189 / 23 / 18 / 0 | 191 / 21 / 18 / 0 | 192 / 41 / 0 / 0 | 182 / 40 / 6 / 2 | 199 / 31 / 0 / 0 | 228 / 2 / 0 / 0      |
| 0.73             | 82 / 89 / 33 / 26 | 209 / 17 / 4 / 0  | 211 / 15 / 4 / 0  | 204 / 29 / 0 / 0 | 189 / 36 / 4 / 1 | 209 / 21 / 0 / 0 | 228 / 2 / 0 / 0      |
| 1                | 208 / 22 / 0 / 0  | 226 / 4 / 0 / 0   | 227 / 3 / 0 / 0   | 221 / 12 / 0 / 0 | 227 / 3 / 0 / 0  | 228 / 2 / 0 / 0  | 228 / 2 / 0 / 0      |
| 1.5              | 82 / 60 / 62 / 25 | 226 / 4 / 0 / 0   | 227 / 3 / 0 / 0   | 220 / 13 / 0 / 0 | 203 / 19 / 5 / 2 | 226 / 4 / 0 / 0  | 228 / 2 / 0 / 0      |
| 2                | 32 / 13 / 21 / 22 | 85 / 3 / 0 / 0    | 224 / 6 / 0 / 0   | 217 / 16 / 0 / 0 | 201 / 18 / 8 / 2 | 224 / 6 / 0 / 0  | 228 / 2 / 0 / 0      |

The two rows per zoom that are not ok under the final rules are not the board's: the engine's own
no-change label commit at the fitted scale and Wardley's 22 px colour swatches. The view battery
(`sync-view`, not in the count above) went from 103 / 53 / 8 of 164 rows (round 4) to
145 / 16 / 3.

1. Decide the rule before the technique: **a model is edited at board zoom exactly 1.** Arming a
   node from any board zoom first moves the board to 100 % at that node while the node is still a
   preview, then arms it. A zoom of the board under an armed editor ends the editing first and
   keeps what was typed. A Done control stays in the toolbar. The reasons are measured:
   - Below zoom 1 no strategy offers usable targets: chrome that scales is 11 to 16 px at 0.5,
     chrome that keeps its size covers or loses content (sections 2 and 3).
   - One state for every engine. Asked at board zoom 0.5 to 2, every one of 1167 rows per run of
     the five editor batteries runs at zoom exactly 1, and a row asked above or below 1 equals the
     same run's zoom 1 row in verdict (section 13). An armed node at another board zoom was seen
     in 0 of 57,975 samples over 990 armings.
   - Only at exactly 1 an armed editor is its preview for every kind of view. "Zoom 1 or above"
     (the default of round 5) leaves an HTML view at 100 % while its preview is scaled: a decision
     table cell jumps 7 / 129 / 191 / 239 / 423 / 1160 px on arming at 1.02 / 1.2 / 1.37 / 1.5 / 2
     / 4. At exactly 1 it is 0 px in 36 of 36 rows, and bpmn-js's no-change commit at editor zoom
     100 % writes nothing at 6 of 6 asked zooms.
   - A board zoom under an open editor (round 4: the picture up to 56.5 % out of scale, a drop
     331 to 698 px from the pointer) cannot reach an editor any more: the node is a preview before
     the first zoom tick in every row, typed text is in the file in 95 of 95 rows (round 5) and for
     six Miragon editors in 30 of 30 per run (round 6), and a held shape refuses the zoom.

   What the rule costs a person, also measured: an arming that moves the board takes about 290 ms
   until the editor takes a press (26 ms when nothing moves). Above zoom 1 the picture shrinks to
   1 / zoom about the pointer, and the zoom a person gained on the board is not the editor's:
   detail zoom has to be the editor's own. A zoom of the board costs the selection and one
   double-click. A decision table in an 800 x 540 node shows 41 % of itself at every asked zoom
   (95 % at 1.5 and 100 % at 2 under "1 or above").

2. Under that rule, embed the armed editor as `overlay`. It is exact for all seven engines and
   touches none of them: no private member, no module per engine, nothing an engine update can
   switch off silently. It costs what every in-page editor costs (473 KB of heap and 21.9 ms per
   further editor, section 5). Its menus may leave the node. At net scale 1 any content works, the
   decision table and whatever a later notation brings included. Under the final rules it has no
   broken and no wrong-data row in the five editor batteries at any asked zoom, and the rules are
   board behaviour: they do not depend on the strategy and patch nothing either.
3. Keep `iframe` as the second choice. It is the only strategy that needs no rule (no broken row
   from board zoom 0.1 to 4, HTML views scale with the board), and its price is known: 3.1 times
   the heap, about 3 times the mount time, menus that end at the frame, crossed writes, two board
   gestures to repair, three pieces of glue to own. It is the answer if editing at any board zoom
   becomes a requirement, or for content that cannot run in the board's page.
4. Do not build on `plugin` or on `shim`. `plugin` answers the question it was built for: one
   module is enough for diagram-js, and diagram-js is not the whole product (the decision table, 20
   renderer-own sites, repairs that lean on private members and fail silently). `shim` proves that
   the problem is one of coordinate space and nothing else, and is no foundation: 13 browser
   functions patched page-wide, virtual answers to board code, one browser. Worth keeping from
   both: the seven rules of section 4 as a conformance run for the Miragon renderers, should
   editing in place at any zoom without a frame ever be wanted.
5. What a product has to decide or build for the rule, beyond what the spike has (section 13,
   "What is still wrong"):
   - A node larger than the board at 100 %. It cannot show both the point a person double-clicked
     and the palette. Keeping the point (as built) leaves 0 of 14 palette entries in 45 of 51 rows
     when the arming comes from an overview; putting the node's top left first (round 5) moves the
     point 193 to 918 px. Both are measured, neither is good. A way around that was NOT built and
     NOT measured: since the armed host lives in a screen-space layer, it need not be the node's
     whole rectangle; it can be the part of the node the board shows, so the editor always fits.
   - The selection is dropped by every disarm and should come back with the next arming of the
     same node. A window resize while a node is armed places nothing again. Done leaves the node
     selected, so Backspace then deletes it from the board. After Done by the keyboard the focus
     falls to the body.
   - `lock` (the board refuses a zoom while a model is edited) is built and measured as the
     alternative to `disarm`. It keeps popups and the selection and needs Done before every zoom.
     Which of the two a workshop wants is a question for a real room, not for a script.
6. Three findings are product work whichever way is taken (sections 6 and 7). A peer's edit closes
   an open label editor and the typed text is lost, which weighs most on a board where an AI client
   edits the same models during a workshop. The board file needs an element-wise merge, coordinates
   that cannot be half written, a text type for a note and a validator rule before two branches
   change it. The board has no undo on the page.
7. What would change this recommendation. A requirement to edit at any board zoom: `iframe`. A
   real room in which the move to 100 % or the shrinking picture above zoom 1 disturbs: "zoom 1 or
   above" (`armzoom=min1`) for notations that have no HTML view is the measured fallback, since a
   diagram view keeps its geometry above 1 (0 px, section 12). Still not measured: a second
   browser, a touch device (a pinch was only emulated), pen and IME, a real room, the board battery
   and the cost run under the rules, the rules on `counter`, `naive` and `shim`, `lock` and `back`
   in the six editor batteries, and any time figure beyond 5 samples per run on a quiet machine.

### 12. The arm rules, measured

This section is the record of round 5: its "default rules" are `min1 / disarm / stay`, and section
13 has the final default (`exact1 / disarm / stay`), the two fixes and what round 6 withdrew here.

Round 5. Section 11 leans on three rules that had not been built: the zoom to the node on arming, a
minimum arm zoom of 1, and a disarm on a board zoom. They are built as board behaviour, the same
for every strategy and switched by URL parameters ("Start the page"), and measured by four kinds
of run:

| Run                                                                                                                       | Rows                                                                                       | Repeats and load                                                                                                                                                                                                                |
| ------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------- | ------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------ | ------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------- |
| The builder's own script, the same steps before and after the change, and an acceptance run                               | 242 of 242 checks; smoke run 126 of 126 cases with default parameters; 67 of 67 pure tests | Single runs at a 1 minute load of 1.7 to 2.7                                                                                                                                                                                    |
| `arm-rules` (13 cases, R1 to R13) on `overlay`, R9 on `iframe` and `plugin`                                               | 723 rows per run                                                                           | Two runs: verdict equal in 723 of 723 rows, measured text in 718 of 723 (the 5 others: the pointer's place in the diagram differs by 15 to 45 units after an auto-scroll with a shape held outside the editor). Load 2.1 to 7.5 |
| The six editor batteries on `overlay`: a control run with the rules off, runs A and B with the default rules, supplements | 135, 182, 125, 178, 445 and 280 rows per run                                               | A equals B in every verdict but one row (below). Load up to 52 (5 to 49, 20 to 52, 8 to 44 for the three pairs of batteries): no time of these runs is cited as a result                                                        |
| An independent check of fourteen claims with scripts of its own, written from the builder's report alone                  | Three fresh pages per number; 216 seeded sequences of 12 steps                             | Load 27 to 49                                                                                                                                                                                                                   |

Every number names the board zoom asked for (the URL's) and, where it differs, the zoom in effect
after arming. Window 2200 x 1300 (board 1860 x 1300, toolbar band 55 px), node 800 x 540 at
(60, 80), device pixel ratio 1, unless a row says otherwise.

#### What was built

| Rule                                             | Behaviour as built                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                              | Parameter                                    | Default (`overlay` node / any other) |
| ------------------------------------------------ | ----------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------- | -------------------------------------------- | ------------------------------------ |
| Zoom to the node on arming                       | The board moves first, while the node is still a preview, and the node is armed in the same task as the last viewport write. The rule's zoom is applied about an anchor (the pointer for a double-click; the node's centre for Edit, the hook and the URL), then the board is shifted by the least amount that puts the whole wrapper into the board's box below the toolbar band minus 24 px. The move is the board's own loop of one viewport write per animation frame, 250 ms long, eased by the design tokens, 0 ms under reduced motion. A node that is in place already is armed at once | `armzoom=min1` or `exact1`; `armmotion=<ms>` | `min1` / `off`; 250 ms               |
| A minimum arm zoom of 1                          | `min1`: a board zoom up to 1.01 becomes exactly 1, a larger one stays. `exact1`: exactly 1 from anywhere. `off`: the board stays as it is                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                       | `armzoom`                                    | `min1` / `off`                       |
| A board zoom under an armed editor disarms first | `disarm`: a zoom the board sees coming (a wheel or pinch on the board, caught ahead of d3-zoom; the toolbar's out, 100 %, in and Fit) disarms synchronously before its first tick, then the zoom runs. A zoom it cannot see coming (a script's `setViewport`) is met inside its first tick. While a press that began in the armed editor is held, the zoom is refused instead. `lock`: every zoom is refused, the four toolbar zoom buttons are disabled. `free`: the board of round 4. A pan never disarms, and a wheel inside the armed editor never reaches the board                        | `armedzoom`                                  | `disarm` / `free`                    |
| The way back (added)                             | `back`: Done, a click on the bare board and the hook's `arm(null)` make the node a preview first and then move the board back to the viewport before the arming                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                 | `armreturn`                                  | `stay` / `stay`                      |

The pure part is `arm-rules.ts` (30 `node:test` cases), the board part `board-arming.ts`. The hooks
gained `armPolicy`, `armState`, `lastTransition`, `transitions`, `whenRest`, `startFrames`,
`stopFrames` and `counters().arm` (`transitions`, `disarmsBeforeZoom`, `disarmsAtTick`,
`zoomsRefused`, `zoomsUndone`, `armedZoomTicks`, `lateDisarms`); `lateDisarms` is the standing
self-check and is 0 in every run of this round. React Flow's animated `setViewport` was measured
first and not used: its promise resolves 411 ms into an undisturbed move of 400 ms and never
settles (2.5 s) once a wheel, a pan press, an instant `setViewport` or `zoomIn` interrupts it.
Besides the rules the build stage fixed the delete guard and the wheel range of `overlay` (section 8).

- Under `min1` above 1 the table is fully usable (R1: a cell written, 11 of 11 list entries at 1.5
  and 2). What differs is the picture: it jumps on arming and
  back on every disarm, a zoom disarm included (`sync-view` arm-disarm: 191 / 239 / 423 px at 1.37
  / 1.5 / 2, all broken; zoom-plain at 1.37: 37 % off after the disarm with 0 armed frames at
  another zoom). The literal expression jumps too: its text field 43 / 59 / 118 px, its properties
  block 166 / 224 / 449 px. At 1.02, the first zoom `min1` leaves alone, cells move 4.9 to 8.6 px
  (independent check, 3 of 3).
- `exact1` from 1.37, 1.5 and 2: 0 px and no differing pixel in 30 of 30 arm-disarm and preview
  rows of `sync-view`, and all 50 `dmn` rows asked at 1.5 and 2 equal the zoom 1 row. Its price:
  41 % of the table in view where `min1` shows 95 % (1.5) and 100 % (2), 1 of 9 entries of the
  table's type list and 2 of 9 of the literal expression's in reach where 9 of 9 were, and a
  landmark that travels 50 to 90 px on arming from 1.37, 68 to 122 px from 1.5 and 135 to 244 px
  from 2 (hook, anchor the node's centre). From zoom 4 by Edit, hook or URL it leaves the node in
  the box's bottom right corner, where the replace menu flips by 370 px (3 rows, 12 of 12 entries
  in reach).
- bpmn-js at a fractional zoom above 1: a label editor closed without typing writes even at editor
  zoom 100 % (label-noop-100 degraded at 1.37, ok at 1, 1.5 and 2). The rule itself leads there: a
  wheel burst outwards from 2 disarms and ends at 1.2834, and arming again arms at 1.2834 with no
  move. From 1.5 the burst ends at 0.9626 and arming again goes to 1.

Attacks of the independent check that changed nothing (3 of 3 each): Control + wheel from editor to
bare board and back, a wheel during a held shape drag (6 of 6 wheels refused, drop 0.6 px off) and
during a held header drag, a script animating the viewport while arming and under an armed node,
keyboard only (2 Tabs to Edit, Enter or Space arms at zoom 1), a 36 node board with the far node at
viewport (-2920, -1964), device pixel ratio 1.5 and 2, the Files panel shown during the move, a
lasso or create tool active, and an emulated two-finger pinch (below).

A double-click on a shape in the lower right part of a node at zoom 3 moves that shape 1811 px, out of the board                                                                                          | Move only as far as needed to cover the box: with `least = low + span - size`, shift by `low - start` when `start > low`, by `least - start` when `start < least`, else by 0. | `nodes/model-node.tsx` (the header's button), with `shiftInto` | Done sits at the header's right end; a node wider than the board is placed with its left edge at the margin, so Done lies outside the board (5 of 8 rows of R4)                                                                                                                                                                                  | Pin the button to the header's visible right end, put it beside the title, or add a Done to the board's toolbar while a node is armed                                                                             |

The view can change while armed (drill-down from the DRD), so a rule per view at arm time would not catch it                                                                                        | Proposed by the `dmn` rerun: `exact1` as the default for `overlay`, or for a node whose file can show an HTML view. Question 3 has the numbers for both                                                           |

The frame also learns of a disarm one task later (not measured)                                                                                            | Let the frame report press and release to the board (a message in the frame protocol of `shared.ts`), or treat an armed frame node as `lock` while its frame has the pointer                                      |

Under `lock` and a held press a script's zoom is undone, not prevented: React Flow's store sees the tick and the undo | None suggested                                                                                                                                                                                                    |

Matters of the harness, not counted against the rules. Each of the six battery scripts had a rule
that used the zoom asked for or the URL's node position, and each is fixed with the rules off
leaving every row as it was: `bpmn-gestures` (one line, the travel of 7 gesture sizes),
`bpmn-chrome` (label-resize, wheel-unarmed, label-noop-100, and wheel-limits, which cannot take
an editor step once the board zoom has disarmed the node), `dmn` (the travel of the DRD move and
the node's corner in switch-views-zoomed), `sync-view` (the arming helper of 16 call sites, the
hook's zoom step, the preview taken again at the viewport in effect), `miragon-a` and `miragon-b`
(one line each, 14 and 6 uses of the pointer's travel). Still open are the verdict rules that
predate the arm rules, the resize handle reported as broken when it is absent, and
`NodeHandle.select` of `lib.ts` (section 8). The smoke run was not adapted: it still takes its
travel from the zoom asked for, so an `overlay` row asked at 0.5 drags 30 x 20 px at zoom 1 in
effect. Its ratio is a quotient and stays 1: 0.983 to 1.015 in 7 of 7 such rows of the builder's
final run (126 of 126 cases measured, none with an unexpected ratio), where the table of section
2 has 0.994 to 1.008 for the drag at board zoom 0.5.

#### Not measured in round 5

- A touch device, pen, IME, a real trackpad's inertia (bursts are 8 or 20 wheel events a frame
  apart), any browser but headless Chromium 158. A real browser's page zoom on Control + wheel or
  on a pinch over an armed HTML view.
- The board battery (`board-chromium.ts`, whose armed part runs on `overlay`), the cost run, and
  the two builders' scripts were not repeated under the rules. What a move costs per frame in
  React Flow's own move callbacks is not measured.
- `armzoom=exact1`, `armedzoom=lock` and `armreturn=back` in the six editor batteries: `exact1`
  ran in `dmn` (50 rows) and in two cases of `sync-view`, the other two only in `arm-rules`, the
  builder's run and the independent check.
- The rules on `counter`, `naive` and `shim` beyond one row each of the builder's run (armed at
  zoom 1 from 0.5, disarmed by a wheel, `lateDisarms` 0); on `iframe` and `plugin` bpmn-js and one
  decision table row only.
- A board zoom under an armed Miragon editor in the batteries: only the label commit of the
  independent check. Event Storming's note editor and suggestion list, Context Maps' prompt, the
  literal expression and the boxed expression under a zoom disarm.
- `sync-view`'s zoom cases at asked 0.5, 0.73, 1.5 and 2; the six compare-board rows with five
  peers (run with one); arming by a real double-click in `dmn` and in the Miragon batteries (they
  arm by the hook, anchor the node's centre).
- Times: the quiet figures are 5 samples (3 for frames) per run in two runs at a load of 2 to 4,
  and the builder's single runs. Every other run was under a load of up to 52.
- Interruptions at the last frame before the arming; a fit by a caller other than the toolbar; a
  peer's edit or a second peer during a move; `armreturn=back` after a window resize; whether a
  key pressed during the move should be queued; Escape while armed.
- Device pixel ratio 2 beyond two rows of R1 and the independent check's geometry; window sizes
  beyond those named; a board zoom between 1.02 and 1.2 for the preview comparison; a table larger
  or smaller than the sample's 1224 x 345 px.
- The wheel range clamp beyond the wheel-limits rows (section 8). The delete guard in a battery.
- The corner supplement ran once, at asked 0.5, bottom right only. How much of the Done button a
  flipped Wardley menu covers there is read off one screenshot (about 25 of 64 px), not hit-tested.

### 13. The closing round: two fixes and the final default, measured

Round 6. Round 5 left three things to close (section 12): a node larger than the board's box was
thrown to its top left corner on arming, the header's Done button was out of reach when the node
was wider than the board, and under `min1` an HTML view armed above zoom 1 was not its preview.
This round changed the first two, made `exact1` the default of an `overlay` node, made the verdict
rules of the old scripts aware of the policy, and measured the result with four kinds of run:

| Run                                                                                                                               | Rows                                                                                                                                               | Repeats and load                                                                                                                                                                                                                                               |
| --------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------- | -------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------- | -------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------- |
| The fix stage's own script, the same real gestures before and after the change, and an acceptance run                             | 153 pages before and 153 after, three fresh pages per number; 222 of 222 checks in 63 cases; smoke run 126 of 126 cases; 81 of 81 pure tests       | A second full run after the change: identical in 51 of 51 cases. Load 0.1 to 7.5                                                                                                                                                                               |
| `arm-rules` (15 cases, R1 to R15) on `overlay`, R9 on `iframe` and `plugin`                                                       | 906 rows per run (round 5: 723)                                                                                                                    | Two runs side by side: verdict equal in 906 of 906 rows, measured text in 902 (the 4 others: the pointer's place after an auto-scroll with a shape held outside the editor). Load 3.7 to 33.6, median 14; the 13 time rows again alone at a load of 1.5 to 2.5 |
| The six editor batteries on `overlay`: a control run with the rules off (C6), runs A6 and B6 under the final default, supplements | 135, 182, 125, 178, 460 and 295 rows per run; a `sync-view` supplement of 360 rows per run at asked 1, 1.37, 1.5 and 2                             | A6 equals B6 in the verdict of 1375 of 1375 rows. Load 18 to 49: no time of these runs is cited                                                                                                                                                                |
| A third independent check of ten claims with scripts of its own, written from the fix stage's report alone                        | About 4,300 pages, three fresh pages per number; 216 seeded sequences of 12 steps, 72 more without a rest between the steps, 36 with the rules off | Load 16 to 49                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                  |

Every number names the board zoom asked for (the URL's) and, where it differs, the zoom in effect
after arming. Window 2200 x 1300 (board 1860 x 1300, toolbar band 55 px), node 800 x 540 at
(60, 80), device pixel ratio 1, unless a row says otherwise. "The default" in this section is the
final one, `exact1 / disarm / stay`; in section 12 it is `min1 / disarm / stay`.

Decisions of the fix stage are in "Behaviour that is a decision, not a result". One of them goes
against the fix's own fixture and is carried through this section: the rule says "an end short of
the high margin goes to it", so a pointer arming still moves a node that leaves a gap.

Which rows changed their verdict against round 5's run A, and why. 37 changed only because a rule
of a script changed; 5 changed by the new default; none got worse:

notations other than bpmn-js; J10 with a Miragon node, with `lock` or `back`, with more than two
nodes; the rules switched on for the other five strategies; any time figure. The side effects of
the two counter-tests (no tooltip on a disabled button, `overflow: clip` in other browsers) are
not measured.

#### Fixed after the batteries

The two defects the independent check found by its sequences (J10) were fixed by the orchestrating
agent after the batteries, each with the one CSS rule the check had injected as its counter-test,
and measured again with the check's own two scripts on the sources as they are now (no injected
rule, three fresh pages per case):

| Defect                                                                                                                    | Fix (`board-spike.css`)                                                                                            | Before                                                                                                                                                                           | After                                          |
| ------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------- | ------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------ | -------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------- | ---------------------------------------------- |
| A drag released over a switched-off toolbar button is never ended (Chromium dispatches no mouseup over a disabled button) | `.board-toolbar :is(select, button):disabled` takes no pointer events, so the release goes to the toolbar's ground | The board or the dragged node follows the pointer in 18 of 48 pages: pan over the Done control 9 of 9, pan over the delete button 6 of 6, node drag over the Done control 3 of 3 | 0 of 48, the page sees the mouseup in 48 of 48 |
| The browser scrolls the overlay layer under a label editor that crosses the board's right edge                            | `.board-overlay-layer` clips without being a scroll container (`overflow: clip`)                                   | The armed host 15 to 16 px beside its node body in 12 of 18 pages (four settings, the rules off included)                                                                        | 0 px in 18 of 18                               |

Regression on the fixed sources, by the orchestrating agent (one run each, 1 minute load 0.4 to
10): the smoke run 126 of 126; `bpmn-chrome` on `overlay` under the final default 182 of 182 rows
identical to run A6 in verdict, headline and every measured number; the cases R4, R5, R6, R13 and
R14 of `arm-rules-chromium.ts` (322 rows) against run A: verdict equal in 321, measured text equal
in 317; a replace menu that reaches over the Files panel (the check of section 10: 58.5 % of it
beyond the board) is still 100 % in view and takes a press at 8 of 8 entries, 3 of 3, so the
layer's new clipping cuts no popup; 81 of 81 pure tests, the gates and the mirror build (identical,
275 files). The one verdict that changed is the first fix's doing: under `armedzoom=lock` a press
on a switched-off zoom button now reaches the toolbar's ground and closes an open popup of the
editor, as any press outside the editor does (R5, popup, lock, toolbar zoom out: ok before,
degraded now). The 4 other rows that differ in text are held-out rows, the auto-scroll variance
named above.

The price of the first fix: a toolbar button that is off shows no title on hover and no cursor of
its own, which under `armedzoom=lock` hides the sentence that says why the zoom buttons are off
(the attribute is still there). A product would use `aria-disabled` and a guard in the handler
instead; that was not built. Not measured for either fix: any browser but headless Chromium.

#### What is still wrong

Apart from the two defects above, nothing here is fixed. A suggested fix comes from the report that
found the matter and is not tried unless the row says so; "none suggested" where no report names
one.

| `board-arming.ts` line 307 (the kind of anchor), `arm-rules.ts` lines 114 to 120 (`shiftInto`, the pointer branch)         | The pointer kind is used for every double-click, also when the arming changes the zoom from an overview: the zoom about the pointer decides which part of a node longer than the box is shown                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                 | Palette 0 of 14 in 45 of 51 rows, header out of reach in 42 of 51; no bare board to pan in 9 of 72 pages                                                           | Use the pointer kind only when the rule leaves the zoom as it is (the case round 5 found), and place by the centre kind when the zoom changes; or close the low side first where the pointer lies within one box length of the node's start |

### 14. `overlay` with the product's own editor

Round 7, the first step from the spike towards the product. Until here every in-page strategy ran
on mounts of the spike's own (`engines/bpmn.ts`, `dmn.ts`, `miragon.ts`), which leave out the
product's key bindings, its todo layer and its menu, and only the embed frame of `iframe` ran the
product's mount functions. With `&mount=product` an `overlay` node now mounts what the SPA mounts
(`mountBpmnEditor`, `mountDmnEditor`, `mountMiragonEditor`, unmodified) and moves that editor's
host into the board's layer while armed (`strategies/overlay-product.tsx`).

| What                                | With the product's editor                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                     |
| ----------------------------------- | --------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------- |
| Product code changed                | None. The mirror build is identical (275 files)                                                                                                                                                                                                                                               |
| Code of this path                   | 302 code lines: `strategies/overlay-product.tsx` 214, `embed/inpage-contract.ts` 37, `embed/product-mount.ts` 51. It uses neither the spike's mounts (355 code lines) nor the view controller (171) nor `engines/use-engine.ts` (166)                                                         |
| Why no view controller              | The arm rules arm an `overlay` node at board zoom exactly 1 and disarm it before a board zoom: the armed host has the size of its preview, so the editor's own view is never rescaled. With the rules off or under `min1` above zoom 1 this path is not defined (the picture is not in scale) |
| Keys of several editors in one page | Every product mount binds its zoom keys on the document and asks `ctx.visibility` whether its canvas shows. The node answers "armed". Five editors in one page, Control + minus and Shift + 1: only the armed editor's view changed in 10 of 10, and none with no node armed in 2 of 2        |
| Ready and release                   | Read off and done through the DOM, as in the embed frame (`embed/inpage-contract.ts`): the product's `MountedEditor` has neither. No row failed because of it                                                                                                                                 |

What this settles and what it leaves:

- The open point of section 13 and of the recommendation ("no in-page strategy was measured with
  the product's mount") is closed for the editors themselves: 1197 rows of the five editor

  mount have one cause, the product's own first fit in a host of the size of a node. The embed
  frame showed the same 5 + 5 rows in round 4.
- Left for the mount contract: a first fit that leaves room for the palette and the cards in a
  small host (bpmn-js, the DRD); "first import done" and "release" as calls instead of DOM
  heuristics; a reaction to a resize of the host.

  presence layer with data, the product's menu and dialogs, the cost run, any browser but headless
  Chromium. `sync-view`'s peer rows need a way to edit a file without an engine handle.

  (`?board=stress&count=2&mount=product`, each of the seven notations). An element dragged in the
  armed node lies at the same place in the other node's preview, within 0.3 px, and the two
  replicas have the same text within 8 ms; then the other way round with the roles changed. 14 of
  14, no console line. This stands in for `sync-view`'s peer rows for one kind of edit only, the
  move of one element.

### 15. The board as a module under the modeler suite's contract

Decided after section 14: the board stays on React Flow. The question that followed was whether
the Miragon modeler suite still has a place for it, since every suite modeler is one notation on
diagram-js. Read in the suite's repository (github.com/Miragon/modeler-suite): only a part of the
suite needs diagram-js.

| Part of the suite                                                                                            | Needs diagram-js                                               | For a board on React Flow                                  |
| ------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------ | -------------------------------------------------------------- | ---------------------------------------------------------- |
| `@miragon/modeler-api`: the contract (text in, text out, a change event, view state) and its conformance kit | no: no runtime dependency, the kit is driven through a harness | usable, measured below                                     |
| `@miragon/modeler-shell`, `./vscode/extension` and the document sync of `./vscode/webview`, `./orca/webview` | no by their signatures: they take an import and an export      | usable as read, not tried                                  |
| `@miragon/modeler-shell`: the chrome's viewport and palette, `mountIdeEditor`, `./diagram-js`                | yes                                                            | not usable as they are: the board has a toolbar of its own |
| the template of the `modeler-kit` plugin and the rule "one notation on diagram-js"                           | a convention                                                   | an exception, if the package ever moves there              |

What this round built, all inside the spike's folder, no product file touched:

| File                                  | What                                                                                                                                                                                                                                              |
| ------------------------------------- | ------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------- |
| `module/collaborative-board.tsx`      | `createCollaborativeBoard({ hub })`: the React Flow board as a `CollaborativeModeler` (attach, import, export, change event, view state, content box, the elements surface). The host brings the files, the board owns document, history and view |
| `module/board-text.ts`                | The board file read strictly. The house codec's decode skips what it cannot read, and the next export would drop it: here such a text is refused as a whole (L7)                                                                                  |
| `module/kit-main.ts`                  | The harness of the suite's conformance kit and two checks of its own, the entry of `board-spike-kit.html`                                                                                                                                         |
| `test/spike/contract-kit-chromium.ts` | Runs that page and prints every case                                                                                                                                                                                                              |
| `test/spike/board-text.test.ts`       | 9 pure tests of the strict reader                                                                                                                                                                                                                 |

`board-context.tsx`, `board.tsx` and `toolbar.tsx` got an embedded mode: a runtime made from a
host's document and files, no Files panel, no board and strategy choice, no `window.__boardSpike`.

The kit of `@miragon/modeler-api` 1.7.0 against the board, 36 cases. The last run (the three before
it had the same verdicts on every case they ran):

| Outcome | Cases | Which                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                         |
| ------- | ----- | ----------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------- |
| passed  | 26    | every case that is a law: L1 (3 of 3, one of them over a note's open editor), L2, L3, L4, L5 (3 of 3, one of them a view change over that editor), L6 and L7; meta; calls before the first import; the four elements cases; `elements.bounds`; `contentBounds`; destroy twice |
| failed  | 0     |                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                               |
| skipped | 10    | 6 recommendations that need a held drag or pan or a focusable canvas (import during a gesture, drop, user action, drag start, pan, destroy from a listener); keys (recommended); and 3 optional surfaces the board does not offer (interaction, appearance, viewer)           |

The first run had 19 passed, 17 skipped and 1 advisory (an import called from a change listener
ran inside the user's command). Imports now start in a microtask of their own and keep their call
order; the advisory is gone. The elements surface brought 5 more cases, the note's editor scripted
as the board's interaction the last 2. No console error in any run. The page's log was empty in
three runs and held one line in the last: Chromium's "ResizeObserver loop completed with
undelivered notifications", not looked into (board.tsx knows the notice from a node resize).

Two checks beyond the kit, in the same page: the model node of an embedded board mounts the
product's editor (bpmn-js with a drawn element), and a double-click arms it at board zoom exactly 1
in the layer above the board, after which a host's `setViewState` to zoom 0.5 makes it a preview
again. Both pass.

Regression of the board page after the embedded mode: smoke 21 of 21 with the spike's mount and 21
of 21 with the product's, 90 pure tests, the mirror build identical (275 files).

What this settles and what it leaves:

- A board on React Flow can be a modeler in the contract's sense. The canvas technology decides
  nothing about VS Code: a custom editor is a webview, and the suite's document plumbing takes an
  import and an export function.
- The real work for an IDE host is the embedded models. A webview cannot show another extension's
  editor, so the board's bundle has to hold every editor it shows live and has to get the linked
  files from the extension. That is the same on diagram-js.
- The contract already names what section 14 found missing in the product's mounts: a free area
  for the first fit (`freeArea`, `fitOnImport`), an import that resolves when rendered, keys only
  while the canvas has the focus.
- Read, not measured: React Flow takes its delete keys from the whole document. The contract
  recommends that a modeler acts on a key only while its canvas has the focus; the kit's keys
  case needs a focusable canvas element, which the board does not have yet.
- Still the spike's: the module loads the spike's samples through `board-context.tsx`, its styles
  come from the page, and the editors come from `embed/product-mount.ts` instead of being handed
  in by the host.

### 16. The board in designIQ: the notation, the editor, the bench apart

Decided: a board is a file with the extension `.board`, notation id `board`. This round is the
first that changes product files. Nothing is committed.

**The platform.** The board is the first structured notation: an element-wise live document,
JSON lines at rest. The lane was built and dark; registering the notation switched it on.

| File                                   | Change                                                                                                              |
| -------------------------------------- | ------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------- |
| `packages/notations/index.ts`          | the descriptor: `board`, `.board`, `docShape: "structured"`                                                         |
| `packages/notations/codecs.ts`         | the house codec registered for `board`                                                                              |
| `packages/notations/templates.ts`      | the blank board: the header line with the title                                                                     |
| `packages/notations/counts.ts`         | a noun that is the label itself is not said twice ("1 board", not "1 Board board"); found by the package's own test |
| `apps/live-host/test/overview.test.ts` | the test that wants a model of every notation gets a board file                                                     |

Unit tests after it: notations 150, validator 86, MCP package 44, live-client 122, live-host 497
(its release script was not run), web 707; type check, architecture rules and lint without an
error.

Two guests on a real Live Host (`LIVE_AUTH=none`, a throwaway content repository), no browser, 12
of 12: both get the file as a structured document, byte for byte; a move reaches the other in about
20 ms; two moves of different nodes at the same time both survive; a resize and a text edit of the
same note at the same time both survive; the host writes the canonical text to the working tree
within about 2 s, one line per element; the REST read returns the same text; a BPMN room beside it
still opens as text.

**The editor in the web app.**

| File                              | What                                                                                                                                                |
| --------------------------------- | --------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------- |
| `src/notations/board/plugin.ts`   | the manifest; the board loads when a `.board` file is opened                                                                                        |
| `src/notations/board/editor.tsx`  | the board on the session's structured document, with the product's editors through the registry                                                     |
| `src/notations/board/live-hub.ts` | the board's files in designIQ: a model it shows is a live session of its own on that model's room, an image comes from the repository's image route |
| `src/notations/registry.ts`       | `EditorContext.linkedFiles`: other files of the repository as live sessions; the plugin is registered                                               |
| `src/components/live-editor.tsx`  | the shell hands `linkedFiles` in, under the person's own presence                                                                                   |

The board's own code stayed in this folder, and the bench moved out of its core: `createBoardRuntime`
takes every part from whoever mounts the board (`BoardParts`: store, hub, surfaces, and for the
spike page alone `bench` with the sample choice, the Files panel and `window.__boardSpike`).
`main.tsx` brings the samples and all six strategies; the product brings the overlay surface with
the product's editors and nothing else. Check: the product build in a mirror WITHOUT the sample
files succeeds, 294 files where it had 275, none of them with a trace of another strategy, the
Files panel or the kit.

The real web app against the local Live Host, 16 of 16 (`app-board.ts` in the scratchpad, one run
after the last change, three before it with the same verdicts):

- the `.board` file opens as a board with its three nodes; the BPMN and the DMN node show the
  product's editors with the models drawn; the toolbar offers the repository's four models and no
  board;
- a drag of a note changes exactly one line of the board file and leaves the BPMN file alone;
- a double-click arms the BPMN node at board zoom exactly 1 in the layer above the board; a drag of
  a task there lands in the BPMN file of its own, the board file stays as it is, and a second page
  that has the same model open on its own shows the task moved;
- Done makes it a preview again; no console error beyond the todos routes a host without a tracker
  answers with 501 (on the second page, the product's own).

The bench after the split: smoke 126 of 126 over all six strategies and 21 of 21 with the
product's mount, the contract kit 26 passed, 0 failed, 10 skipped as before, 90 pure tests.

Left before this is a pull request:

- The board's code is still under `src/board-spike` with the bench's names in its classes and one
  bench module in the product build (`native-geometry.ts`). It moves to `src/notations/board`.
- Seen in the app: the first fit puts the board under the toolbar's band in a low window; the
  product's own first fit cuts a model's left edge in a node (section 14's finding).
- Keys: React Flow takes Delete and Backspace from the whole document (section 15).
- One socket per model node. The host allows 40 per address (`LIVE_MAX_WS_PER_IP`): a board with
  more models than that needs sessions that share a socket.
- A person who has a board open counts as present in every model it shows.
- No image upload from the board (the host has a route to read an image, none to store one), no
  todos on a board, no history diff, no read-only view, and the model list names a board by its
  file stem, not by its title.
- The documentation that lists the notations (the repository's CLAUDE.md, `docs/`) does not name
  the board yet.
- Not measured: more than one board user in the app (the two-guest check has no browser), a board
  with many models, any browser but headless Chromium.

### 17. The board's code in the product folder, and keys that stay on the canvas

**The move.** The board's own code left this folder: 32 files went to `src/notations/board/`
(the core flat, `nodes/`, `edges/`, and `contract/` for the module of section 15), six pure
tests to `apps/web/test/board-*.test.ts`, where the product's test command runs them (792 tests
there now, 707 before). What stayed here is the bench: the spike page's entry and samples, the five
strategies that are not shipped with their engines, the in-page hub, the Files panel, the test
hooks, the kit's harness and this README. The bench imports the board from the product folder and
never the other way round (no import of `board-spike` under `src/notations`, `src/components`,
`src/routes` or `src/lib`).

What had to come apart for that:

| Was shared                   | Now                                                                                                                                                                                              |
| ---------------------------- | ------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------ |
| `shared.ts`                  | the board's types in `notations/board/types.ts` (notations, boxes, the arm rules' vocabulary, the probe, `BoardOptions`); `shared.ts` re-exports them and keeps the hooks and the frame protocol |
| `model-hub.ts`               | the interface a board asks its host for in `notations/board/files.ts` (`BoardFiles`); the in-page hub implements it and adds what only the bench uses                                            |
| `samples.ts` (`BoardParams`) | `BoardOptions` in the core; the bench's parameters extend it                                                                                                                                     |
| `native-geometry.ts`         | the board reads the screen through `notations/board/geometry.ts` (plain DOM calls); the bench swaps in the functions it captured                                                                 |
| `board-spike.css`            | `notations/board/board.css` (1165 lines) for the board, 465 lines here for the strategy label, the Files panel, the embed frame's page and the counter strategy; the root class is `board-root`  |

**Keys.** React Flow listens for its keys on the document. Measured with the contract kit's keys
case as a control (`SCOPE=page`, React Flow's own listeners): Backspace, Delete and Space aimed
at a host's editor beside the board (a Monaco editor, an EditContext editor) were prevented.
`BoardParts.inputScope` decides whose a key, a paste and a dropped file are:

- `"board"` (the default, what a host gets): React Flow's delete, multi-selection, pan and zoom
  key codes are off. The pane that holds the nodes takes the focus with a click on the board or a
  Tab into it, and answers Delete, Backspace and, new, Control or Command + Z, Shift + Z and Y
  (`undoRedoKeys`). A press that holds Control or Command adds to the selection, read off the
  press itself. A paste and a dropped file are the board's only inside it, and a moving board
  stops only the keys aimed at it. The pane and not the board's root takes the focus: the layer
  of an armed editor is a child of the root, and a press on a part of that editor that cannot
  take the focus has to leave the focus on `<body>`.
- `"page"`: as before. The bench uses it for the five strategies whose armed editor stays in its
  node, and `"board"` wherever every model node is an overlay node, so the batteries measure what
  ships.

Two more changes in the same round: a note's editor and the inline input of a label compare with
the text they OPENED with, so an editor nobody typed in no longer writes its old text over a
peer's change; and a host's board fits clear of the toolbar (`fitPadding`, 88 px at the top), the
bench keeps the fit it was measured with.

### 18. Presence, one socket, and links that follow a model

Three things section 17 left before a pull request. All of it is product code; the bench is
untouched but for the hub's types.

**One socket for a board's models.** `@designiq/live-client` got `openLiveSessionGroup()`:
several sessions of one client over one socket (Hocuspocus carries any number of documents on a
connection, each authenticated on its own). The shell opens one such group per editor for
`ctx.linkedFiles`. Against the local Live Host, 14 of 14: three rooms of one client open one
socket, each holds its file (the board as a structured document), an edit reaches a second client
in about 20 ms and stays out of the other rooms on the socket, and destroying one session leaves
the others syncing. In the app the board page holds two sockets, its own and one for both models.

**Present only while editing.** A linked session is opened without announcing the person;
`linkedFiles.present(session, true)` announces them while the model's node is armed, `false`
takes it back (`LiveSession.setUser(null)`). In the app, on a second page that has the model
open on its own: the roster shows that page's person alone while the board only shows the model,
two people while it is edited on the board, one again after Done.

**Links.** A board names a model by its path. Like a call activity it now emits one reference
per model node (`@designiq/notations/refs`, relation `shows`, by notation and file stem), which
gives it the platform's existing machinery:

| Event                                                | What happens                                                                                                                                                        | Checked                |
| ---------------------------------------------------- | ------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------- | ---------------------- |
| a model is renamed, the board open                   | the host rewrites the board's live document (one line), the open board's node gets the new name and shows the model from its new file                               | in the app             |
| a model is renamed, the board closed                 | the host rewrites the board file                                                                                                                                    | on disk                |
| a model is moved to another folder, the board closed | the file keeps the old path and the reference still resolves by name: the validator does not warn. The board, opened again, shows the model and writes the new path | in the app and on disk |
| a model is moved or deleted, a board has it open     | the host refuses (409, "open in a live editing session"), as it does for any open model                                                                             | over HTTP              |
| a model the board shows is deleted                   | the validator warns: `board node n_credit shows dmn 'credit-check', which is not a model in this repo`                                                              | validator CLI          |

21 of 21 in the app run (`links.ts` in the scratchpad). For this the strict reader of the board
file moved from the web client to `packages/notations/board.ts`, where the validator and the host
reach it: a board text its canvas could not read back is an ERROR (`board/parse`). Checked over
HTTP: a save with a cut-off line or a repeated id is refused with 422, where the codec's total
decode alone would have dropped the line silently; a save that links a model that is not there is
taken with a warning.

After it: notations 163 tests, validator 89, MCP package 44, live-client 122, live-host 497 and
its offline release script 92 of 92, web 783 (the reader's 9 tests moved to the notations
package), VS Code extension 15; type check, architecture rules, lint and formatting without an
error; the validator's and the MCP package's builds succeed. The app run 21 of 21, the two-guest
run 12 of 12, the contract kit 28 / 0 / 8, smoke 126 of 126 and 21 of 21.

The README, `docs/mcp-integration.md` and the repository's CLAUDE.md name the board now; ADR 0006
has an amendment.

Left:

- The refusal to move or delete a model that a board merely shows: a board holds a live session
  on every model it shows, so "close it first" now also means "close the boards that show it".
  The message does not say which board.
- A model renamed while the board is closed AND the rename's rewrite of the board failed stays a
  dangling link until someone picks the model again; the validator names it.
- The product's own first fit cuts a model's left edge in a node (section 14).
- No image upload from the board, no todos on a board, no history diff, no read-only view; the
  model list names a board by its file stem.
- Not measured: more than one board user in the app, a board with many models, any browser but
  headless Chromium.

### 19. Live presence: cursors and selections

Asked for after a look at the app: no cursors showed. Nothing had been built for them. The board
neither published a pointer nor drew a peer, and the embedded editors were mounted without the
presence surface the product's canvases use.

| Where                            | What is drawn                                                                                        | Coordinates | Who is drawn                                            |
| -------------------------------- | ---------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------- | ----------- | ------------------------------------------------------- |
| the board (`board-presence.tsx`) | the cursor with the name chip, an outline around each selected node; an AI client's last save dashed | the board's | everyone who has this board open                        |
| a model on the board             | what the product's own editor draws (`lib/presence-canvas.ts`), in the preview too                   | the model's | everyone who edits that model, on a board or on its own |

This person's cursor goes out on the board's room while the pointer is over the board, and on a
model's room only while that model's node is armed; inside the armed editor the board cursor is
taken back, so one pointer is one cursor. Presence comes from the host (`BoardParts.presence`,
`LinkedFile.presence`); the bench and the contract module have none.

The web app against a local Live Host, two pages on one board (A acts, B watches) and a third
with one of its models open on its own (C), 25 of 25 (`cursors.ts` in the scratchpad, the second
run; the first failed two rows because the script read the cursor before it had come to rest):

- B shows A's cursor at the spot A points at, to the pixel, with the name on it; A does not see
  its own. After B zoomed out the cursor lies on the same spot of the board and its glyph keeps
  12 x 17 px.
- B outlines the note A selected.
- A arms the process: B's board cursor of A goes, B's PREVIEW of the process shows A's cursor on
  the task A points at, and C shows it too. The task A selects is outlined in both.
- After Done nobody is drawn in the model on B or C. With A's pointer off the board B shows no
  cursor; after A closed, no outline.

After it: the app run 21 of 21, the contract kit 28 / 0 / 8, smoke 126 of 126 and 21 of 21, web
unit tests 783; type check, architecture rules, lint and formatting without an error.

Left: an arrow someone selected is not outlined (it has no box of its own); a DMN model on a
board shows no cursors, as the product's DMN editor shows none; presence was run with one
person's identity on every page (a host without authentication), so colours per person were not
seen.

### 20. A first fit that leaves the editor's own chrome free

Section 14's one cause of the 22 worse rows: bpmn-js and the DRD of dmn-js fit their diagram edge
to edge (diagram-js's `fit-viewport`), under their own palette and name card, which a canvas of a
node's size has no room for. In the app the left edge of a pool lay on the node's edge.

`SyncOptions.fitInsets` (`@designiq/live-client`): the px the host's chrome covers along the
canvas's edges, read when the FIRST import fits the view; `fitDiagram()` then fits into what they
leave free, never zooming in past 1, as `fit-viewport` does. Absent, the editors fit as before:
the standalone editors do not pass it. The board passes it for BPMN (beside the palette) and DMN
(beside the palette, below the name card), each with a 12 px margin, measured from the editor's
own DOM (`product-mount.ts`, `chromeInsets`). The Miragon renderers fit clear of their chrome by
themselves.

Measured against section 17's run (one run each, default rules, the product's editors):

| Battery         | Before       | Now          | Changed rows                                                                    |
| --------------- | ------------ | ------------ | ------------------------------------------------------------------------------- |
| `dmn` (125)     | 115 ok / 10  | 125 ok / 0   | 5x `drd-definitions` and 5x `drd-open-table`, degraded to ok                    |
| `bpmn-gestures` | 130 ok / 5   | 130 ok / 5   | none (`resize-participant`: the canvas scrolls during the drag, as before)      |
| `bpmn-chrome`   | 171 ok / 11  | 171 ok / 11  | none in verdict; the cut context pad entry of the 640 x 388 node is another one |
| `sync-view`     | 120 / 3 / 55 | 120 / 3 / 55 | none                                                                            |

No row is worse. Four unit tests pin `fitDiagram`'s arithmetic (live-client 126 tests). Seen in
the app: the pool starts right of where the palette stands, the DRD below its name card.

Left of section 14's list: the context pad at a small node's edge, the scroll while a participant
is resized, the 8.3 px text of a Team Topology label editor; a resize of the node does not fit
again.
